Before that, 29 Sep 2026 — **Graph Transformer: the 3 puzzle-authoring mismatches found
by the earlier par-verification pass (below) are now fixed, and that pass is a permanent CI
check.** A-level #7 and #25 both targeted amplitude ×3, unreachable with only the ±2ⁿ y-scale
buttons (Stretch ×2/×½ y + Reflect x-axis) — retargeted to amplitude ×2 on Jon's ruling: #7 is now
`y = 2(x+1)² + 2` (par 3, unchanged), #25 is now `y = 2sin(2x) − 1` (par 3, unchanged); each
puzzle's `desc` (and hence "Show steps", generated from the target) was updated to match. A-level
#24 (`y = −2(x−1)² + 3`) keeps its target; its par is corrected 3 → 4 — reaching `a=−2` from `a=1`
needs Reflect x-axis AND Stretch ×2 y, two separate buttons with no single click doing both, so the
`a` dimension alone costs 2 moves, not 1. New `scripts/verify-graph-transformer.py`, wired into
`.github/workflows/check-site.yml`, reads `window.GT_VERIFY` back from the live page
(`games/graph-transformer/index.html` gained `transformButtonDefs()` — factored out of
`buildTransformButtons()`, same button set, same behaviour — plus a `gtBfsCheck`/`gtCurveKeyPairs`
verification hook; no button, match-check or move-counting logic touched) and, for every puzzle in
every tier, runs a breadth-first search using that tier's ACTUAL buttons and the ACTUAL
`curvesMatch()`/`evalState()` comparison. FAILs on: an unreachable target, a stored par that does
not equal the BFS minimum, or two puzzles sharing an identical target curve. Confirmed locally: #7
and #25 each solve in 3 moves at par, #24 in 4 at par, and all 50 puzzles across both tiers are
reachable and correctly parred except the one tracked exception below. A fault-injection self-test
(clones a puzzle, forces amplitude back to 3; clones another, mutates par by +1) runs every CI pass
and both are caught; the same two faults were also hand-injected directly into the file, confirmed
the verifier FAILed on each, then reverted — transcript kept in `docs/snagging-list.md` snag #7.
`check-site.py --tier all`: 348/0/0, network guard clean; `--tier 3 --only graph-transformer`:
UNSUPPORTED, 0 FAIL, unchanged. Tier 4 layer A ledger unaffected (`check-banks.py --ci`: matches
the ledger exactly, 95/95).

**One further mismatch, found by the new duplicate-curve check, is NOT one of the three named

Sep 2026: track it, don't fail CI on it, fix later.** GCSE #11 "Reflect in x-axis" and #12 "Reflect

any PR number or commit hash from before 2 Oct 2026 refers to `OrthogonalMaffs/maffsgames-archive`
(archived, read-only). This repository's own PRs are #1–#36 so far.

**A live bug is never a decision item, even when its fix needs a call.** It goes at the top of
this file as a bug, filed under §1. Expectation Station's stage-1 lock sat for three days under
"Needs Jon's decision" while other work went ahead — the wrong list for a defect that has been
silently failing every session since 22 March. A decision item is something with no wrong answer
until Jon picks one; a live bug is wrong now, whatever the fix turns out to be.

Sections, in priority order. **Nothing in §4 starts until §1–2 are clear.**

| 1.7 | **Chart Interrogator, cumulative-frequency Phase 2: the spread key can never vary.** It compares the IQR with 1.5 × itself (`sessionAnswers.iqr < (q3 − q1) × 1.5`), so the key is "relatively consistent" in all 10 scenarios and the model answer repeats it. Its other dropdown, "25% have a value below Q1 / above Q3", offers **two true options** and accepts only "below Q1". **RULED 26 Sep:** C (a benchmark question built from the scenario's existing `benchVal`) + D (a per-scenario IQR comparator keyed on the scenario's true IQR, replacing the constant ×1.5 self-comparison). Contract B: docs/next-contract-chart-interrogator-cumfreq.md. **FIXED 28 Sep 2026 (`d8b244e`).** The spread sentence is replaced by a benchmark question (a typed count, marked to ±2.5% of n) and an IQR comparator keyed on the drawn curve's true IQR. The verdicts split 5/5, every compIQR/IQR ratio lies outside 0.7–1.4, and CF7's comparator is £280 by Jon's ruling. The 25% dropdown now offers below Q1 / the median / Q3, with only Q1 correct. No Phase 2 key reads `sessionAnswers`. Record: `docs/audit-chart-interrogator-cumfreq.md`. | — fixed |
| 1.9 | **Chart Interrogator marks accurate readers wrong.** The key uses straight-line interpolation between class boundaries; the chart itself is drawn as a Bezier curve, so a student reading correctly off the curve is marked wrong against the straight-line value (CF6 Q1: 185 drawn vs 192.5 keyed; CF7 Q3: 1546 drawn vs 1575 keyed). Expected values are also rounded oddly (CF9 Q1: 1.53 → keyed 2). The fixed ±3 tolerance IS the bug. Fix: tolerance = 2.5% of the axis span (IQR: 2 ×), key from the drawn curve, no rounding of expected values. Class across chart types, not just cumulative frequency. Contract B: docs/next-contract-chart-interrogator-cumfreq.md. **FIXED for cumulative frequency, 28 Sep 2026 (`d8b244e`).** One curve definition, `cfCurve`, now feeds the drawing and every key. The tolerance is 2.5% of the axis span (5% for the IQR), and keys are unrounded. In all 10 scenarios the key matches the pixel-measured drawn curve to within 0.05% of the axis, and the drawing is pixel-identical. The rest of the class, box plots and stem and leaf, is 1.16. | — fixed (cumfreq); rest → 1.16 |
| 1.10 | **Component Crusher renders prose through `K()` (KaTeX math mode), so it loses its spaces** — e.g. "Whatisthereversetranslation?". **FIXED 27 Sep 2026.** Root cause: `component-crusher`, `factor-theorem` and `test-the-claim` all passed a whole mixed prose+maths string to `K()`/`katex.renderToString()` in math mode, which has no concept of a word boundary. Fixed at the shared layer: **`schools/assets/mathtext.js`** (`MaffsText.html()`/`MaffsText.hasMaths()`) splits a string on `\( \)`, HTML-escapes the prose, and renders only the delimited maths through KaTeX — a string with no `\( \)` at all is treated as plain text, not sent through KaTeX blind. Each game got a `PQ()` wrapper (`hasMaths(s) or no LaTeX command at all → MaffsText.html; a bare LaTeX command with no \( \) → still K()`, the old-style whole-expression case) and had its flagged prompts rewritten to wrap only the maths: `component-crusher` (37 strings), `factor-theorem` (39, which also converted its Unicode exponents/minus signs — `x³ − 6x²` → `\(x^3 - 6x^2\)` — clearing its own canon §7.1.1 debt in the same strings), `test-the-claim` (3). `:207`'s ambiguity fixed in the same edit: "What is the reverse translation?" → "The arrow shows a translation. What translation takes the arrowhead back to the start?". B7 checked for two new failure modes too — unbalanced `\( \)` delimiters and a segment KaTeX can't parse — both with their own fixture tests (`check-banks.py --selftest` / `--selftest-live`). **B7 = 0 platform-wide, ledger re-recorded, tiers 1+2 and check-banks --ci green.** Canon §7.1 now names `MaffsText` as the required renderer for mixed strings. | — fixed |

**Deferred when the repo was rebuilt for public release (2 Oct 2026). Do these first, in this order.**
The work was left out of the fresh repo's first commit and is held as patch files outside the repo.

1. **Wrong-keys batch 1 (stat-attack, graph-sketcher, growth-and-decay): reapplied from patch 0001
   (old `2e1ffe5`) in PR #3. DONE: merged 3 Oct 2026 (`9f9ce59`), live pages byte-identical to the merge.** Corrects the keys in the three games, including the
   three growth-and-decay keys ruled under §1.36 (`:229` → 1800, `:380` → 15500, `:404` → 2170), and adds
   `scripts/verify-stat-attack.py`, `verify-graph-sketcher.py` and `verify-growth-and-decay.py`. Each one fails on
   the uncorrected game and passes on the corrected one, and all three now run in CI. Patch 0001 holds no
   tax-theft work; an earlier version of this item said it did.
2. **Reapply contracts-into-docs (APPROVED by Jon, 2 Oct 2026)** (PR #68 on the old repo: the wrong-keys batch 1 and
   Fermi Lab rebuild contracts as `docs/next-contract-*.md`), with the Fermi wheel-revs question's start city changed to Bristol (Jon's ruling; its id changes to match).
3. **Review Firebase database rules (leaderboard write access) and consider HTTP-referrer restriction
   on the web API key; Jon to make the GCP change.** Context (2 Oct 2026): the repo is public, and
   GitHub secret scanning flagged the Firebase web config `apiKey` (alert #1, closed as won't fix: public
   by design, access controlled by the database rules). The rules are `firebase/database.rules.json`.
4. **Tax-theft: DONE (3 Oct 2026, branch `claude/tax-theft-keys`).** Four faults fixed in
   `games/tax-theft/index.html`. (1) **Band method:** income tax was charged with the basic band
   stretched to a gross £50,270, so every salary over £100,000 was under-taxed by £500 to £2,514. Tax is now
   on taxable income: 20% on the first £37,700, 40% to £125,140, 45% above (gov.uk 2025/26). The step's
   wording teaches band widths on taxable income. (2) **Medium:** £105,000 and £118,000 needed the taper
   medium never teaches; replaced by £86,500 and £97,500. (3) **Keys:** every key comes from one function
   (`computeKeys`), in whole pence, from the figures the payslip shows; only net monthly pay rounds (nearest
   penny, half up; no ties in any salary), and its step says so. (4) **Closing line:** "lost your personal
   allowance entirely" only at £125,140 and above; below that it names the reduced allowance. Answers are
   checked to the money-answer standard (canon §7.1.3). `scripts/verify-tax-theft.py` (CI, group B)
   plays all 20 salaries; it failed on the old game (489 FAILs) and passes on the new one.
5. **Apply the money-answer standard (canon §7.1.3) to every money game, and decide whether it belongs in
   a shared helper.** The 'whole pounds' ruling was misread as "round answers to pounds"; it means
   whole-pound amounts need no .00. §1.36 is corrected; tax-theft is the reference implementation (a
   game-local `moneyResult()`). Games with a typed number input and £ on the page, NOT yet classified as
   having money answers: `chart-interrogator`, `graph-sketcher`, `growth-and-decay`, `split-it`,
   `stat-attack`, `test-the-claim`. `split-it` is the known live case (§1.36: ±0.05 accepts wrong pence).
   A shared `schools/assets/` helper is §1.36's proposal; the decision is Jon's.
6. **Check other games that compute income tax for the gross-threshold band misconception** (the basic
   band treated as ending at a gross £50,270 whatever the allowance). Search of 3 Oct 2026 (every game and
   escape room, for income tax / personal allowance / £12,570 / £37,700 / £50,270 / tax band / payslip):
   only `core-maths-paper1`. `:383` uses the gross framing ("20% on earnings above this up to £50,270")
   but with a full allowance, so its answer is right; no question there tapers. Separately, `:447` and
   `:489` state NI at 12% (the employee rate has been 8% since 6 April 2024; 2025/26 is 8%). Report only;
   not changed.

**Handover, 2 Oct 2026 (home, the theme pilot). Pick up here.**

  - **Canon §7.5 / §7.5.1** replace the three-row table and the lowest-tier rule with ONE register
    (Outfit text, JetBrains Mono numbers, OpenDyslexic only in Aa; no emoji in `<h1>`; age-neutral
    copy; each game its own `--accent`), with Jon's reason and the resit-strand reasoning folded in.
    **Palette rule:** roster levels include Year 6, KS3 or GCSE → `data-theme="light"`; only A-Level,
    A-Level Year 2, Further, Core, L3, L4 → dark (Core dark by Jon's ruling). Derived from the roster,
    never chosen: `palette()` in `scripts/check-theme.py` is the only copy. 67 light, 30 dark.
    CLAUDE.md's Theme section, the roster's resit-strand note and `.claude/rules/project-claude.md`'s
    design summary now point at canon.
  - **`schools/assets/theme.css`**: base tokens (`--bg --surface --surface-alt --text --muted
    --border --correct --wrong --hint --font-text --font-num`), a light palette (white surfaces, navy
    `#1a2744` text, beside the portal) and a dark one, chosen by `data-theme` on `<html>`; it loads
    both fonts. **Aa wins on font**: `body.accessible` switches both font tokens to OpenDyslexic, so
    every element written with them follows. **Contrast measured, all AA**, table in canon §7.5:
    lowest light 5.20 (`--hint` on its tint), lowest dark 5.24 (`--wrong` on `--surface-alt`). Light
    `--correct` was first `#15803d`; on its own 10% feedback tint that was 4.38, so it is `#166534`.

  - **What changed.** (1) `clear()` cancels the control's fallback and floor timers and its listener; a new `wrong()` on the same mount clears the old one first. (2) **Advance once:** `onAdvance` runs at most once per `wrong()`, whatever triggers it (click, Enter/Space on the focused control, a game calling the returned `advance()`, the fallback). (3) **44px** minimum height and width (was 41px tall), with the top margin cut from 14px to 11px so the control's footprint is unchanged and no game fitted to the phone rule moves. CI caught that the first push did not: `free-daily-pizza`'s verifier, which checks Next on all 518 items at three sizes, found three screens with Next ending at 528 against a 528 fold at 320×568; with the margin given back its worst case is 525px again, the same as on the old control, and the verifier passes. (4) **A 3-second floor** (Jon's ruling, 29 Sep): Next is disabled, dimmed, with a faint fill crossing it once (dimmed only under reduced motion), and nothing but the fallback can advance; the fallback and any game's `fallbackMs` run on their own clock. Focus moves to Next when the floor ends. Canon §7.6 records all four; canon §7.6.1 records Jon's ruling on when the asking screen may scroll.
  - **Test: `scripts/test-next-control.py`, now in CI** (a browser test of the helper on a bare page, ~20s). 15 checks, all PASS on the new helper; **9 FAIL on the old one**, including the batch 1 shape — a game's own Enter handler advancing and calling `clear()` advanced **twice** (the old fallback fired later). In three real games at 380px wide (`formula-plug-in`, `modular-battle`, `better-value`): Next 108–113 × 44px, disabled for the floor, Enter at 1s did nothing, Enter after the floor advanced exactly once.
  - **The 15 games on `MaffsNext`:** `better-value`, `differentiation-duel`, `distinctly-average`, `formula-plug-in`, `free-daily-pizza`, `index-laws`, `integration-duel`, `log-laws`, `modular-battle`, `new-shapes`, `prime-or-composite`, `quadratic-factoriser`, `six-sevens-bruv`, `suvat`, `trig-worms`. Read for the contract's STOP IFs: none relies on the fallback firing after `clear()` (`distinctly-average` sets a 24h `fallbackMs` precisely so it never fires); none advances by its own key handler except the two guarded games below. Tier 3 across the whole site (old helper, then new, same code, same deterministic network per `docs/sandbox-checks.md`): **0 FAIL both times**; totals 204 PASS / 0 WARN / 114 UNSUPPORTED before, 198 / 3 / 117 after. Per run, the **44 runs of the 15 games on `MaffsNext` are identical** (every PASS still played 10 of 10 questions). **LATEST, 30 Sep 2026 (night): Free Daily Pizza built, the resit strand's second game and the 97th (item 3.6). Merge on green.** Contract: `docs/next-contract-free-daily-pizza.md` (Jon's, verbatim).

- **The game.** `games/free-daily-pizza/`: fraction, decimal and percentage equivalence, adult register (GCSE+ row), KS3 + GCSE on the portal and on `/op/`. The engine is `window.FDP`; the UI draws only through it.
  - Four stages, enumerated from parameter tables: S1 benchmarks 90 (15 values × 6 conversions), S2 206 (eighths, twentieths, hundredths × 6, plus 68 simplifying), S3 138 (fraction or percentage of an amount, whole-number answers), S4 84 ("A out of B" as a simplest fraction and as a percentage).
  - Values are integer pairs throughout. Recurring decimals are shown recurring, thirds of a percent as a stacked mixed number.
  - Four options through `MaffsOptions.build()`. Each distractor is a named misconception. None equals the answer in value, except one `unsimplified` on questions that ask for the simplest form.
  - A wrong answer shows the true statement, what was picked and why it is wrong, the working, then pictures drawn exactly (pizza up to 24 slices, 100-square grid only for whole squares, bar only for whole parts), then Next. No picture before answering.
  - Practice 20/40 through `MaffsSession`: a single stage, or all four (a quarter each, easiest first).
  - **Only mixed practice ranks (Jon, 30 Sep 2026, before merge).** Mixed-stage practice submits to `practice-q20` / `practice-q40`. A single-stage run submits to **no board**: it goes to `MaffsScoreHistory` (and its personal best) under `stage-s1-q20` … `stage-s4-q40`. Its analytics mode is that same key, and its end screen says "Stage practice isn't ranked; play a mixed round to get on the board." `FDP.boardFor()` returns `null` for it, and `finish()` submits only when there is a board. The verifier plays stages 1–4 and asserts that `submitScore` is never called, that history and the personal best sit under `stage-sN-q20`, and that the line shows. A tenth injected fault (a stage run submitting to `practice-q20`) must FAIL, and does.
  - Daily pizza: 3+3+2+2 questions in stage order, drawn by mulberry32 seeded with FNV-1a of `free-daily-pizza:<UK date>`, no two about the same value in a stage; board `daily-YYYY-MM-DD`. Today's best shows as a target through `standing()`, unmodified; with Firebase blocked, no target and no error.
  - Count-up timer feeding canon 7.3. Analytics: level `all`, mode `practice-q20` | `practice-q40` | `daily`.
- **Jon's rulings during the build.**
  - `GAME_NAMES` takes the game's row.
  - **Every fraction on screen is stacked and big enough to read, never "1/3".** KaTeX `\dfrac` everywhere, including the third in 33⅓% (was `\tfrac`). The HUD reads "3 of 10", not "3/10". The menu uses KaTeX or words, not glyphs. With KaTeX blocked, an HTML stacked-fraction fallback draws every fraction. The verifier fails on any slash fraction or fraction glyph in visible text.
  - **The verifier must not trust the engine.** It reads only parameters, displayed strings, tags and id lists. Every answer and distractor value comes from parsing the display (text and TeX separately) and comparing with `fractions.Fraction` computed from the parameters. The daily set comes from a Python port of the PRNG.
- **The verifier**, `scripts/verify-free-daily-pizza.py`, is in CI with `--require-katex`. It reports 518 items, 1,554 distractors recomputed from their tags, and 760 pictures counted in rendered markup. It recomputes the daily pizza for 400 dates, plus a fresh page, and checks the UK date either side of both clock changes. It also checks seeded practice sets.
  - It plays practice stages 1–4 at 20 (none submits) and mixed at 20 and at 40 against a Firebase stub, choosing the right option by parsing its label. The stub's log shows the keys `practice_q20`, `practice_q40` and `daily_2026_10_01`, apart.
  - The daily pizza is played twice on a simulated UK date: the same 10 both times, stages 1112223344, target 640 shown.
  - One run has Firebase and KaTeX both blocked: no target, no error, stacked fallback.
  - Phone: all 518 wrong-answer screens at 320×568, 375×667 and 390×844. The lowest Next bottom edges are 525 (fold 528), 601 (fold 627) and 599 (fold 804).
  - **Fault injection, 10/10 caught:** a wrong answer, a value-equal distractor (0.750), a wrong tag, a broken daily mix, a daily not easiest-first, a recurring answer shown as 0.33, a lossy pizza, a pre-answer picture, a fraction written with a slash, and a single-stage run that submits.

    - 13 items with only 2 distractors (a ÷100 slip added);
    - practice sets with two neighbours about the same value (the spread now swaps backward too);

    - the HUD's "9/20".

  - `extract-banks` + `check-banks --ci`: 97 games, matches the ledger exactly;

- **For Jon to look at (decisions I made, not ruled):**
  1. **Tags beyond the contract's six.** The contract names six tags. Amounts and "out of" questions needed more: `raw-part` (¾ → 3%, 15 of 60 → 15%), `subtract-instead` (18/24 → 3/9), `one-side-only`, `one-part-only` (⅗ of 45 → 9), `no-divide` (→ 135), `complement` (→ 18), `part-to-part` (15 of 60 → ⅓), `subtract-percent` (25% of 80 → 55). Each is defined in `FDP.TAGS` and recomputed in the verifier.
  2. **How the contract's examples map to tags.** ¼ → 0.4 and ⅕ → 0.5 are the same rule (the denominator read as the decimal), so both are `denominator-as-decimal`. `digit-reading` is the reverse direction: 0.4 → ¼, 25% → 1/25.
  3. **Resolved before merge:** only mixed practice ranks (above). Stage history keys (`stage-s1-q20` …) have no `levelLabel()` name, so the hub's device-local "Your Scores" shows them raw. A label pattern would be a one-line addition, but it was not asked for.
  4. **Option order varies by device.** The daily questions and options are the same everywhere; only the option order is shuffled per device.
  5. **Replays** inflate daily scores (allowed by the contract).
  6. **`game_abandoned`**, fired by `analytics.js`, carries no `mode` (a shared file, not touched).
  7. **The 320×568 margin is 3px.** Longer working text on the simplify items would break it; the verifier will say so.
  8. **Aa mode was not measured** (canon 7.6.1's known gap).
  9. **`MaffsNext.clear()` leaves its 20s fallback timer running.** The game guards against it; the shared helper is worth a contract.
  10. **The shared site footer overflows at 320px** on every game.

**EARLIER, 30 Sep 2026 (late): Normal Navigator, the picture no longer gives the answer away, and Q38 draws honestly. Merge on green.** A follow-up to PR 26's label leak, in the same class: a visual that announces the answer before it is given.

- **The leak.** On a question whose answer is a shading boundary (find z, find k, the continuity-corrected z), the shade edge sat on a labelled μ±kσ tick. Q0 and Q1 ("find z", answer 2) were shaded from the μ+2σ tick. Q4 (answer 0) put a marker window round the μ tick. On the others the boundary fell between two labelled ticks and could be read off the gap.
- **The fix is general, in the draw logic.** `boundaryAnswer(q)` decides from the key and the shade alone (no per-question flag): the key's last number equals a shade boundary as x, or as z, within half its last digit + 0.03. Such a question draws **no tick marks or tick labels** until `showExplain`, then redraws with all seven. It hides all seven, not just the boundary's: one gap between labelled neighbours still names the value.
  - `drawBell` gained one `hideTicks` argument.
  - `drawCurrentBell(hideTicks)` factors out nextQ's region mapping so `showExplain` can redraw.
  - This is the same pre/post switch as PR 26's `hideParams`, not a restructure.
  - It applies to Q0, 1, 2, 4, 18, 19, 20, 21, 24, 27, 35, 41, 42 and 44. The shading stays.
- **Q38** asks about men and women but drew only the men's curve. `drawBell` scales every curve to the same height, so two honest curves at true scale would need a restructure. They would also show the answer: the taller curve at 168 *is* the answer. So Q38 now carries `noCurve` and draws no curve, with the line "Two distributions: compare them below", and names both distributions in the info line.
- **Verifier extended.**
  - A pre-answer tick label on a shading boundary, or inside a narrow marker window, FAILS if its value (as z or as μ + kσ) is the answer.
  - A boundary-answer question (spec kinds `z`, `inv`, `invz`, `cc_z`) FAILS if any tick shows before answering, or if all 7 are not back after. That cross-checks the game's own `boundaryAnswer`.
  - A two-distribution question FAILS unless it draws both curves, labelled, or none.
  - Two new self-test faults: 13/13 caught.
  - **On the pre-fix build it FAILED 19 times across 15 questions**: Q0 and Q1 "tick μ+2σ marks a shading boundary, that is the answer 2"; Q4 "tick μ marks …, answer 0"; the tick count on all 14 boundary-answer questions; Q38 "1 curve drawn", before and after answering. On this build: PASS, 0 FAIL.
- **Question content and answers are untouched.** `noCurve` is drawing data.
- Screenshots checked by eye: Q0 before answering (no ticks) and after (all seven), and Q38.
- **Checks:** verifier PASS (13/13 faults); tiers 1+2 476/476 + tier 2 PASS; `--tier 3 --only normal-navigator` 4 PASS / 0 FAIL, 40 questions (one earlier run failed in 2 s with 0 played, straight after the tiers 1+2 run; two re-runs were clean, consistent with the known `serve-stubbed.py` back-to-back port issue); `check-banks --ci` matches the ledger exactly.

**EARLIER, 30 Sep 2026 (night): Normal Navigator, every question recomputed (§1.26, first of four games). Merge on green.** Same fix pattern as Test the Claim.

- **The verifier.** `scripts/verify-normal-navigator.py` runs in CI. It reads the `QUESTIONS` literal statically with esprima, including any `QUESTIONS[n] =` patch, which is itself a FAIL. It reuses nothing from the game's maths: its `phi()` is never called. Each question's meaning is written in `SPECS`, keyed by its exact `ctx`: the kind, the region or conditions, and only numbers printed in the question. An edited or new question FAILS until its spec is written. The distribution is parsed from the prose (`N(50, 8²)`, `B(200, 0.45)`). A second argument without ² that is not `1` FAILS as σ-vs-σ² ambiguous.
- **What it checks.**
  - **Keys:** every key against `NormalDist`, to within half a unit of the key's last printed digit.
  - **Inverse questions:** the keyed μ/σ must satisfy **every** stated condition at its printed precision.
  - **Options:** no two options equal; no distractor equal to the answer at its own precision (for an exact answer, at the key's precision).
  - **Drawing:** the drawn μ/σ, and a solved μ must be the answer's. Every shade bound must sit within 0.025 sd of the true boundary, and a two-tail region needs both tails.
  - **Worked steps:** every "a = b ≈ c" and "a > b" chain in steps, misconceptions and prompts. Φ, φ, √ and `P(X < a)` are evaluated. The student's arithmetic on the printed numbers must round to the next literal. Every other number must match something derivable from the question.
  - **Hygiene:** check-banks' B6 draft markers plus "actually", "closest", "recheck", "..."; no duplicates (textual or same maths); no `correct_override`.
  - **Rendered page:** then it **renders every question on the real page** (stub server, Playwright). Before answering, the μ/σ labels must not show any answer number that isn't in every option. After answering they show μ and σ. The page draws as many shaded regions as the bank asks for.
  - **Self-test:** 11 injected faults, run first on every invocation. The contract's three faults were also injected into page copies, and each FAILED:
    - Φ(2) keyed 0.9773
    - Q29 keyed "μ = 22, σ = 2", which gives P(X < 20) correctly but P(X > 35) = 0.0000 against the stated 0.0228
    - Q31 drawn at μ = 60
- **Found on the untouched bank: 11 FAIL across 9 of 47 questions** (over the 10% STOP; Jon ruled to proceed). The full stated → corrected list is in §1.26. Headlines:
  - Q18: k 60.2 → **60.3**.
  - Q23: 0.0456 → **0.0455** (convention: calculator values throughout, Jon).
  - Q37 relied on an unseen previous question. It is now self-contained, with key 45 → **46**.
  - Q38 needed "Assume equal numbers of men and women".
  - Three worked subtractions did not close (0.9772 − 0.0228 is 0.9544, not 0.9545). Now 5 d.p. intermediates, 4 d.p. result.
  - Both `correct_override`s folded into `correct` (both keys were right). `handleAnswer`'s `q.correct_override||q.correct` is unchanged and still works, with `correct` always set.
- **Game changes beyond the bank** (Jon's ruling 6):
  - `hideParams` on Q29, 30, 31, 34, 39 and 45 makes the labels read "μ = ? σ = ?" until `showExplain`. They printed the answer before.
  - `drawBell(mu, sigma, regions)` takes a list, and `shade` may be `[[L,R],[L,R]]`. Q23, 29, 33, 37 and 46 now shade both regions (Q37 was a two-tail region too).
  - No other UI, flow, scoring, levels, leaderboard or analytics change.
- **Checks:**
  - Verifier: PASS, 0 FAIL, 11/11 faults caught.
  - Tiers 1+2: 476/476 PASS, tier 2 PASS, network guard clean.
  - `--tier 3 --only normal-navigator`: 4 PASS, 0 FAIL, 40 questions played.
  - Tier 4: full extract + `check-banks --ci` matches the ledger exactly across 96 games. Only normal-navigator's entry changed: B10's two entries retired, layer A `verified`, hand-edited, not `--write-ledger`.
  - Screenshots of Q23 (both tails) and Q31 ("?" labels) checked by eye.

  1. ~~The axis ticks gave a boundary answer away.~~ FIXED: see the LATEST block above.
  2. ~~Q38 drew only the men's curve.~~ FIXED: see the LATEST block above.
  3. §1.26's other three games remain: `regression-rumble`, `core-maths-paper2a`, `maths-court`.

  | 390 × 844 | 899 / 982px, **0 of 144** | 538 / 602px | 804px | **144 of 144** |

  Screenshots, taken from the top of the page (what a student sees without scrolling), are in `docs/screenshots/`: `six-sevens-wrong-before-6x7-*`, `six-sevens-wrong-after-6x7-*` and `six-sevens-wrong-after-12x12-*` at each size, plus `six-sevens-asking-grid-collapsed-320x568.png`.
- **What changed** (`games/six-sevens-bruv/index.html`, layout only):
  1. On a **wrong** answer the keypad is hidden and the feedback takes its space. It stays for a right answer, which shows one line for a moment; hiding it there would make it vanish and come back under the student's thumb on every right answer.
  2. At 600px wide or narrower, the grid collapses to a bar, "12 / 78 gold" with a fill, that opens and closes on tap (`aria-expanded`, 44px tall). It starts collapsed each session. The results screen still shows the grid in full.
  3. **Order: fact → hint → array → Next**, as ruled. The old screen ran fact → array → hint. "You said" and "comes back in a few questions" are now one line. Dots are 8px on narrow screens, and every dot is drawn.
  4. On a screen 600px tall or less, the array moves below Next. It is a fixed CSS rule, so a phone always gets the same layout, and it changes the visual order only. **The hint is never below Next.** Of the three sizes, it applies at 568 only.
  5. **One change beyond the five listed, flagged:** during play on narrow screens, the Aa toggle stays on the title line instead of wrapping under it. That saves 46px, and without it 375 × 667 reached only 100 of 144. The title keeps clear space on both sides, and the check found no overlap. The menu screen is unchanged.
- **Checks:**
  - `verify-six-sevens.py` PASSES, all five fault injections CAUGHT; its content checks are untouched.
  - Order checked on every measured screen: 0 violations.
  - No horizontal scroll at 320px, with the grid open or closed.
  - The grid bar opens and closes in every run.
  - No page errors.
  - Tier 2 passes. Tier 1's local FAILs are this sandbox's blocked CDNs, so CI is the authority.
- **STOP IF: neither fired.** The hint never moved below Next. No tap target was shrunk: keys are still 46px at 320 and 54px at 375, and the new grid bar is 44px.
- **Canon §7.6.1, the phone rule**, is written with the method. Free Daily Pizza's contract now points to it.

**EARLIER, 30 Sep 2026: Test the Claim, every answer key recomputed (§1.14 FIXED, §1.26 NEW). Built, verified and merged by the PR that carries this note. Jon has TWO things to review, below.**

**The finding.** `test-the-claim` hand-typed every probability, critical value, p-value and verdict, and nothing ever
recomputed them. The new verifier, `scripts/verify-test-the-claim.py`, runs in CI on every push. It uses nothing from the
game's maths: exact binomial via `Fraction`, 60-digit Poisson, `NormalDist`, and the exact null distribution of r. It
found **16 of 48 items wrong**, over the 10% STOP IF, and Jon ruled to proceed. Reading the step code then found two
marking bugs affecting many more items. So the verifier now also runs `buildStep4` and `buildStep6` in a node vm to read
what they mark correct, and holds each marked answer against its own recomputed verdict.

**What students were getting wrong:**
- **P7's decision was backwards.** The true P(X ≤ 9) is 0.0433, so H₀ is rejected.
- **Step 6 marked "reject the claim that ⟨H₁⟩" correct on all 24 items that reject H₀**, contradicting the model answer
  printed straight afterwards.
- **Two-tailed items in the p-value route told the student to compare with the full level**, which marked B3 and P3 wrong
  for doing so.
- **All 24 Normal and correlation items showed "undefined"** in the p-value route and in the hint after a wrong critical
  region.

Every item that changed, stated → corrected, is in **`docs/test-the-claim-verification.md`**.

**Rulings applied (Jon, 30 Sep):**
- Two-tailed items compare **the probability in the tail with half the significance level**, labelled that way.
- Step 6's action is always "…support the claim that…".
- A blank Lower box means "no lower critical region".
- A needed probability that isn't shown is a WARN, with a calculator line in the scenario (7 items).
- PMCC values match the exam booklet. Edexcel 8ST0/9ST0 Table 8 was checked cell by cell against exact computation:
  260/260 agree. The rows the game uses are now in the verifier, and five wrong cells were corrected to them.

**Jon to review:**
1. **The 24 generated p-values and critical-region explanations** for the Normal and correlation items (the doc's

**EARLIER, 29 Sep 2026: Distinctly Average, the answer-format leak. Built, verified and merged by the PR that carries this note. ONE family is left tracked, not fixed, and needs Jon's ruling (below).**

The problem: on bimodal items the correct answer ("3 and 7") was the only option written "x and y", so its
format gave it away (42 of 256 KS3 items). The class: any family whose correct answer differs in shape from
its distractors (the only pair, the only decimal, the only option with a unit). It had no test.

**The check (`scripts/verify-distinctly-average.py`, runs in CI on every push).** Each option is reduced to a
format signature: every run of digits becomes `#`, everything else is kept ("3 and 7" is `# and #`, "6.3" is
`#.#`, "12" is `#`). An item FAILS when the correct option's signature is unique among its options AND the
distractors share a shape it lacks. Two readings of the contract worth knowing: (1) digit RUNS collapse to one
`#`, so "12" and "7" have the same format (length is not format); (2) read literally, "the correct signature
is unique" would also fail every categorical item ("Set A" / "Set B" / "They are the same" each have their own
signature), so a family passes when all its options have different shapes, because nothing singles anything
out. A `SIGNATURE_ALLOWLIST` (family -> pinned leak count and reason) exists for tracked families; a leak
beyond the pinned count is new and fails, fewer is stale and fails.

**Before / after (run on the unfixed data first, as instructed).**

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| KS3 leaking items | 43 of 256: `modeBimodal` 42/42, `median` 1/55 | **0 of 259** |
| GCSE leaking items | 49 of 395: `modeBimodal` 42/42, `median` 1/55, `freqMean` 6/20 | 6 of 398: `freqMean` 6/20 only (tracked) |
| KS3 items / distinct parameter sets | 256 / 256 | 259 / 259 |
| GCSE items / distinct parameter sets | 395 / 395 | 398 / 398 |
| Misconception tags with a recompute function | 33 | 35 |

- **Bimodal (the named target).** Options are now the correct pair, one wrong pair and two single values (2 pairs +
  2 singles on every item), so the correct answer is never the only "x and y". The pair is always written
  smaller-first, like the correct answer. Two new tags, alternating by item: `mode_and_wrong_value` (a true mode paired
  with a value that is not one) and `picked_extreme_values` (the two largest distinct values, or the two smallest when the
  modes are the two largest). The single-value `only_one_mode` and `mode_as_frequency` distractors are kept. Each new tag has an
  explanation in the game and a recompute function in the verifier. Cost: the enumeration now needs at least one non-modal
  value (`n - 2r >= 1`), so the "exactly two values, each repeated equally" shape (6 values, 3 and 3) is no longer generated.
- **Median (1 of 55, found by the check).** An even-n item shows three of four candidate distractors, the dropped one rotating
  by item index; dropping the whole-number candidate could leave three decimals against a whole-number answer. The drop now steps
  to the next candidate if that would single the answer out (`dropOneCandidate`, at most four tries over a fixed list).
  **Unrequested side effect: `median` went from 55 to 58 items.** Three items were being discarded because two of their
  distractors coincided (unsorted median equal to the mean); the new rotation happens to drop one of the pair, so they survive.
  All three pass the recompute check.
- **`freqMean` (6 of 20 = 30%): NOT FIXED, per the STOP IF (over 25%, "redesigning that family is Jon's call").** The correct answer
  is the only decimal, because `S / rows` and `sum(values) / rows` both come out whole when the sum divides by the row count.
  Pinned in `SIGNATURE_ALLOWLIST` at exactly 6 so it cannot spread unnoticed. **A tested proposal**, applied to scratch copies and
  reverted, not committed: when neither of those two distractors is a decimal, replace the second with a new tag
  `averaged_frequencies` (the mean of the frequency column, N / rows, never a whole number here because rows is 3, 4 or 6 and
  N is 10). Result of the trial: `freqMean` leaks 6 to 0, the verifier passes with its own self-test, and `freqMean` grows
  from 20 to 24 items (four items that were being discarded for coinciding distractors survive). The code:

```
    const wrongN = roundStr(S / r.k, 1), ignored = roundStr(sumDistinctOnly / r.k, 1);
    const noDecimal = wrongN.indexOf('.') === -1 && ignored.indexOf('.') === -1;
    const dists = [
      { val: wrongN, misconception: 'wrong_n' },
      noDecimal ? { val: roundStr(N / r.k, 1), misconception: 'averaged_frequencies' } : { val: ignored, misconception: 'ignored_frequency' },
      { val: String(S), misconception: 'sum_not_divided' }
    ];
    // plus in MC: averaged_frequencies: 'You averaged the frequency column. The mean uses each value, counted as many times as its frequency.'
    // plus in the verifier: @tag("averaged_frequencies") -> {round1(Fraction(sum(freqs), len(freqs)))}, and delete the SIGNATURE_ALLOWLIST entry
```

**A residual leak this check exposed and could not close on its own (also Jon's call).** The two mode families share one
prompt, and after this fix bimodal items carry pair options while unique-mode items do not. So the option list still tells the
student which family they are in, and so whether the answer is one value or two (from 100% certain before, down to a 50/50
between the two pairs, but still a cue). A second, cross-family check (`check_shared_prompt_cues`) now detects it, and it is
tracked in `SHARED_PROMPT_CUES` with the reason so any new cue, or this one disappearing, fails CI. Closing it means adding
named pair distractors ("thought it was bimodal") to the unique-mode family too, which touches a second family, so it was not done.

**Fault injection (captured).** One bimodal item was made deliberately leaky in the real game file (its wrong pair swapped for a
single value), the verifier run, the file restored byte-for-byte (same checksum) and re-run clean:

```
FAIL: ks3/ks3_modeB_201: distractor '4' is tagged 'wrong_value_picked' but that mistake gives nothing on this item
FAIL: ks3/ks3_modeB_201: format singles out the correct answer '1 and 3' (signature '# and #', no other option has it); the distractors share '#'
FAIL: gcse/ks3_modeB_201: distractor '4' is tagged 'wrong_value_picked' but that mistake gives nothing on this item
FAIL: gcse/ks3_modeB_201: format singles out the correct answer '1 and 3' (signature '# and #', no other option has it); the distractors share '#'
  ks3   items leaking: 1 of 259   {'modeBimodal': '1/42'}
  gcse  items leaking: 7 of 398   {'modeBimodal': '1/42', 'freqMean': '6/20'}
4 FAILURE(S)
```
```
(after reverting)
Format-signature check (digit runs -> '#'; correct option must not be the only one of its shape):
  ks3   items leaking: 0 of 259   none
  gcse  items leaking: 6 of 398   {'freqMean': '6/20'}
  tracked, not failed: freqMean leaks on 6 items (pinned at 6)
PASS
```
The verifier's own self-test also gained four guards (all caught): a synthetic item whose only pair is the correct answer, one whose only
decimal is the correct answer, a categorical item that must NOT raise a false alarm, and the shared-prompt cue with no allowlist:

```
  format leak     only pair among singles    '1 and 3' -> singled out CAUGHT
  format leak     only decimal among wholes  '6.3'    -> singled out CAUGHT
  no false alarm  categorical Set A/B/SAME            ->          OK
  shared-prompt cue modes told apart by shape           ->          CAUGHT
```

**EARLIER, 29 Sep 2026 (PR #13, merged as `7db590a`): Distinctly Average polish. Built and verified, and merged by the PR that carries this note; the LEADERBOARD IS WIRED BUT HELD, and that needs Jon's ruling.**

What changed, all in one PR:
- **Mode hint on every mode question.** Unique and bimodal, both tiers, now show the identical prompt
  "What is the mode of this data set? (There may be more than one value.)". The verifier asserts one
  prompt across all 75 mode items (both tiers).
- **Compare questions have three options.** "They are the same" is the third, tagged as a named
  misconception on unequal items (`assumed_means_equal`, `assumed_ranges_equal`) and made a live answer
  on 3 of 18 items per family (16.7%) built with equal means or equal ranges by construction. The
  distractors on those items are `compared_wrong_stat`, `consistent_means_higher`,
  `compared_means_not_ranges`, `lower_mean_more_consistent`.
- **GCSE outliers: 48 items, 3 types of 16** (`outlierId`, `outlierMean`, `outlierCompare`), 8 high and
  8 low each, 48 distinct parameter sets, GCSE only. **Construction rule for "clearly separated"**
  (asserted by the verifier from every item's raw data): 5 to 9 whole values in 1..50; the other n-1
  values span S with 3 <= S <= 6; the outlier lies g beyond the nearest of them with
  **g >= max(3S, 10)**. Judged by eye; no 1.5 x IQR test anywhere. g >= 10 is what makes
  |change in mean| > |change in median| true for every item (mean moves >= g/n >= 10/9, median <= 1).
- **Type (c), the effect on the RANGE of removing the outlier, was dropped, deliberately.** Removing
  the maximum or minimum can only shrink the range, so the answer is "goes down" on every item and the
  other two options are dead. Suggested replacement if wanted: "what is the range once the outlier is
  removed?" with a numeric answer and the original range as the named-misconception distractor.
- **`outlierCompare` alternates its wording** ("less affected" answers The median, "more affected"
  answers The mean), 8 items each, because a constant answer would be learnt as a pattern. The prompt
  therefore varies with the answer on this one family, on purpose; the verifier asserts the mapping.
- **The verifier now recomputes every distractor from its misconception tag** (`TAGS` in
  `scripts/verify-distinctly-average.py`: 33 tags, exact `Fraction` arithmetic, a tag with no function
  fails the script, every tag needs an MC explanation). 14 tags were added (5 compare, 9 outlier).
  Result: PASS, ks3 256 items / 256 distinct sets, gcse 395 / 395.
- **Parents `averages` guide:** exactly one sentence added after the median worked example ("If the
  position ends in .5, the median is halfway between the two values either side."). `verify-parent-guides.py`
  still 353 checks, 0 failed.
- **Portal cards** (KS3 and GCSE): `data-badge="updated"`, `data-badge-date="2026-09-29"`. Note the game
  carried "New" earlier the same day; it now reads "Updated".

**DfE GCSE S4.3 (now in the repo, not only in commit text).** `data/dfe-gcse-parts.json`
`statements[94].parts[2]`, tier standard: "appropriate measures of central tendency (median, mean, mode
and modal class) and spread (range, including consideration of outliers". Distinctly Average now covers
mean, median, mode, range **and outliers** (Foundation, by eye). **Modal class is not covered there;
grouped data lives in Stat Attack.** Recorded on the spec-map row. The KS3 National Curriculum citation
(DFE-00179-2013, p.9, bullet 1) comes from the earlier build's commit and was not re-checked.

**THE LEADERBOARD IS WIRED BUT HELD. Jon must rule (STOP IF, reported).** The game calls
`MaffsLeaderboard.submitScore(SLUG, level, score, total, 'q' + total)` and records `MaffsScoreHistory`,
mirroring `quadratic-factoriser` / `better-value`, and it is **removed from `EXCLUDED`** so the shared
coverage script now enforces it. But the call is gated behind `LEADERBOARD_MODE_READY = false` and
declared in `HELD`, the same mechanism `estimation-golf`, `equatle` and `log-laws` use. Why: the
student picks 10, 20 or 30 questions, scoring is 10 per correct answer, and the board key is
`slug_level` only, so a 30-question run (up to 300) would outrank every 10-question run (up to 100)
on one board. Measured: a scripted 30-question session scored 200, a 10-question one 70.
**This is a class, not a one-game bug:** `quadratic-factoriser`, `better-value`, `spot-the-muppet`,
`expected-damage` and `expectation-station` already rank different session lengths together. The
architectural fix is `docs/next-contract-leaderboard-modes.md` (session length as the mode; the gated
call already passes it). Options for Jon: (1) wait for the modes contract and lift the gate then;
(2) accept the platform status quo for this game and lift the gate now (one word), knowing 30-question
runs will dominate; (3) rank one fixed length only, which changes the contract's "keep the scoring".
**Whichever is chosen, lifting the gate also needs `'distinctly-average':'Distinctly Average'` in
`GAME_NAMES` (`schools/assets/firebase-leaderboard.js`) and a row in the `GAMES` list of
`leaderboards/index.html`, both outside this contract's file list** (without the first, the ticker
would show the slug).
Leaderboard counts recomputed from the repo: 95 roster games, **93 call `submitScore()`**, 2 excluded
(`constructions-lab`, `given-that`), 4 carry the gate (`estimation-golf`, `equatle`, `distinctly-average`
submit nothing; `log-laws` submits its Laws drill only), 89 submit unconditionally. CLAUDE.md and canon
now say so; they said 92 of 95, and canon's section 9 had been one short since before this game.

shown / distinct prompts): mean 4/1, median 4/1, modeUnique 4/1, modeBimodal 4/1, range 4/1,
freqMean 4/1, freqMedian 4/1, missing 4/1, compareMean 3/1, compareRange 3/1, outlierId 4/1,

**Fault injection (captured; this is the verifier's own self-test, which now runs on every CI pass).**
One distractor is mutated in every question family, then two distractors' tags are swapped (values
untouched: the "wrong tag, so the explanation blames the wrong mistake" failure this check exists
for), then the mode prompt, the outlier separation, the mean-versus-median inequality and an unmapped
tag are broken. Every mutation must be caught or the script fails:

```
Fault-injection self-test (one distractor mutated per family; each must be caught):
  compareMean     compared_wrong_stat        'A'      -> 'Ax'     CAUGHT
  compareMean     tag swap                   compared_wrong_stat <-> assumed_means_equal CAUGHT
  compareRange    range_bigger_is_better     'A'      -> 'Ax'     CAUGHT
  compareRange    tag swap                   range_bigger_is_better <-> assumed_ranges_equal CAUGHT
  freqMean        wrong_n                    '6.3'    -> '7.3'    CAUGHT
  freqMean        tag swap                   wrong_n  <-> ignored_frequency CAUGHT
  freqMedian      position_not_value         '6'      -> '7'      CAUGHT
  freqMedian      tag swap                   position_not_value <-> mean_not_median CAUGHT
  mean            sum_not_divided            '10'     -> '11'     CAUGHT
  mean            tag swap                   sum_not_divided <-> value_not_mean CAUGHT
  median          unsorted_median            '4'      -> '4.1'    CAUGHT
  median          tag swap                   unsorted_median <-> position_not_value CAUGHT
  missing         wrong_n                    '0.5'    -> '1.5'    CAUGHT
  missing         tag swap                   wrong_n  <-> missing_equals_mean CAUGHT
  modeBimodal     mode_as_frequency          '2'      -> '2.1'    CAUGHT
  modeBimodal     tag swap                   mode_as_frequency <-> only_one_mode CAUGHT
  modeUnique      mode_as_frequency          '2'      -> '3'      CAUGHT
  modeUnique      tag swap                   mode_as_frequency <-> wrong_value_picked CAUGHT
  outlierCompare  mean_thought_robust        'The mean' -> 'The meanx' CAUGHT
  outlierCompare  tag swap                   mean_thought_robust <-> all_averages_equal CAUGHT
  outlierId       picked_smallest            '19'     -> '20'     CAUGHT
  outlierId       tag swap                   picked_smallest <-> picked_neighbour CAUGHT
  outlierMean     mean_direction_reversed    'Goes up' -> 'Goes upx' CAUGHT
  outlierMean     tag swap                   mean_direction_reversed <-> mean_unaffected CAUGHT
  range           range_as_max               '8'      -> '9'      CAUGHT
  range           tag swap                   range_as_max <-> range_as_min CAUGHT
  mode prompt     hint dropped on 1 item              ->          CAUGHT
  outlier rule    outlier pulled 6 closer             ->          CAUGHT
  mean vs median  median moves more                   ->          CAUGHT
  unmapped tag    new tag, no function                ->          CAUGHT
```

**Quadratic Factoriser rebuild — ALL THREE PHASES DONE.** `docs/next-contract-quadratic-factoriser.md`
(dictated by Jon 27 Sep 2026) is the full brief; `docs/quadratic-factoriser-spec.md` is item 0, the
same contract verbatim plus the rulings made on its own drafting error (section 2: the ≥40 floor is
per level, not per stage, and DOTS's 12-item exhaustive population needs no exemption once the
level's pool is deduplicated as a whole).

- **Phase 1 (`14fed9f`):** the 'gcse' (a=1) level rebuilt to the proposed new-game gate standard —
  three-level structure with `?level=`, every mfg()/submitScore() using the real level (fixing the
  old hard-coded 'gcse'), `MaffsSession`/`MaffsNext`/`MaffsText`/`MaffsOptions` all adopted, dark
  GCSE+ theme. Pool: 45+72+45+12 = 174 (both-positive/one-negative/both-negative/DOTS).
- **Phase 2 (`38294ed`):** the 'higher' (a>1) level and its fading Tutorial → Guided → Independent
  AC-method tutorial (spec item 3). Pool: 752-item base + 3008-item HCF (k=2..5) stage, every item
  with exactly one unordered {m,n} AC-split and no non-HCF item carrying a hidden common factor.
  Both named misconceptions (multiplies-to-c-not-ac at step 3; wrong-signed factor breaking the
  bracket match at step 5) fire on trigger; promotion after 2 correct Guided, demotion after 2 wrong
  Independent, "Show me the method" not counting as a fail; Independent marked by expanding and
  comparing a,b,c, never by string.
- **Phase 3 (`e8c7663`):** the 'formula' level (spec items 2, 4) and item 6 (roster/portal/meta).
  Pool: 1070 real-root items + 184 curated negative-discriminant items (every 3rd of the natural
  550, a deterministic thinning — not a random sample — bringing 34% natural negative share down to
  14.7%, matching the spec's "~15%"; ~80% of real-root items have a non-square discriminant,
  matching "mostly non-square"). Items within 1e-6 of a 2 d.p. rounding boundary are filtered at
  build time (none occur in this parameter range, but the filter runs regardless). Answer: two
  inputs (any order) plus a separate "No real solutions" button; marked by rounding both the true
  roots and the entered values to 2 d.p., never by string. All four named wrong-method
  misconceptions (used +b not −b; divided only √ by 2a; divided by a not 2a; squared a negative b
  as negative) fire on their exact trigger, confirmed individually. Roster row, portal card and the
  game's own meta description rewritten to describe the three real levels;
  `data/check-ledger.json` refreshed via `check-banks --write-ledger` (byte-identical — the game
  already correctly read as a generator; see the layer-A note below).

Two real, self-inflicted bugs were found and fixed during Phase 2, both worth remembering for any
future work on this file: (1) several `mx()` call sites (both 'higher' and 'formula' code) started
out wrapping pure-prose strings with no `\( \)` delimiters, so `mx()`'s own documented fallback sent
them whole through KaTeX and glued every word together ("Thecommonbracketcomesoutthefront...") —
exactly canon's B7 hazard class, hit twice more while writing Phase 3 before the pattern was fully
internalised. Fixed each time by not routing pure prose through KaTeX at all; both 'higher' (3760
items) and 'formula' (1254 items) had every step/message string bulk-rendered and scanned for any
15+ letter unspaced run afterwards (zero hits both times). (2) The pool-holding constants were
originally named in SCREAMING_SNAKE_CASE (`HIGHER_STAGES` etc.), which matched
`scripts/extract-banks.py`'s live-bank-detection heuristic and made the checker misread the whole
enumerated pool as an authored question bank (the same false-positive shape already documented
there for `estimation-golf`'s `roundQuestions`) — renamed to lowerCamelCase throughout (`gcsePool`,
`higherPool`, `formulaPool`, ...), confirmed back to `"generator": true` in
`data/banks/quadratic-factoriser.json`. Also fixed: `game_completed`'s `accuracy` was inferring the
correct-answer count from `score / 10`, which only worked because 'gcse' scores 10/correct —
'higher'/'formula' score 15/correct and the old formula reported 150% on an all-correct 'higher'
run; now tracked directly via a `correctCount` variable shared by all three levels.

Verified per phase: `verify-quadratic-factoriser.py` PASS for all three levels (SymPy-checked
expansion/roots, no duplicate (a,b,c) per level, per-item AC-split/common-factor/rounding-boundary
checks); scripted stub-server playthrough to `game_completed` at every offered session length for
gcse (8/16/24/174) and formula (8/16/24) in full, and for higher (8/16/24) in full plus the
mode-transition into Independent confirmed at the "All" (3760) length — the full 3760-question
run was not played to completion in real time because each Independent answer's own 1.4s advance
delay makes that ~90 minutes of real game time, not because the code path differs from the
8/16/24 runs already proven correct; all six named misconceptions across both levels confirmed
firing with their exact wording; tiers 1+2 green throughout (344 PASS, 0 FAIL, 0 WARN); tier 3
`--only quadratic-factoriser` UNSUPPORTED throughout (recorded in `checker-allowlist.json`, same
multi-step-picker shape as `suvat`/`prime-factorisation`); `check-banks --ci` green throughout.
Screenshots of each level's first question, every 'higher' tutorial step, misconception messages
on both levels, and a formula worked solution are on file (not committed to the repo).
